Appendix A
Momentum-space Feynman rules for Yang–Mills theory

For convenience, we collect here the conventions and elementary
momentum-space Feynman rules used throughout the main text. We work in the
radial coordinate
z
, with
k=|\bm{k}|
denoting the boundary energy
associated with a spatial momentum
\bm{k}
. The external bulk-to-boundary
propagator is

K^{i}(z)=\bm{\epsilon}^{i}e^{-zk},

(A.1)

while the transverse bulk-to-bulk propagator is written in spectral form as

G^{\perp}_{ij}(z_{1},z_{2};k_{s}):=\Pi_{ij}^{(k_{s})}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{\sin(pz_1)\sin(pz_2)}{(k_{s}^{2}+p^{2})},\qquad\Pi_{ij}^{(k_{s})}=\delta_{ij}-\frac{k_{s}^{i}k_{s}^{j}}{k_{s}^{2}}.

(A.2)

The longitudinal propagator is local in the radial coordinate:

G^{\parallel}_{zz}(z_{1},z_{2};k_{I})=\frac{\delta(z_{1}-z_{2})}{k_{I}^{2}}.

(A.3)

Each bulk vertex is accompanied by an integration,

\int_{0}^{\infty}dz.

(A.4)

Three-point vertices.

Latin indices denote spatial components, while a
z
index denotes the radial
direction. With all momenta taken outgoing, the cubic vertices that enter our
computations are

\displaystyle V_{ij\ell}(\bm{k}_{1},\bm{k}_{2},\bm{k}_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Bigl[\delta_{ij}(\bm{k}_{1}^{\ell}-\bm{k}_{2}^{\ell})+\delta_{j\ell}(\bm{k}_{2}^{i}-\bm{k}_{3}^{i})+\delta_{\ell i}(\bm{k}_{3}^{j}-\bm{k}_{1}^{j})\Bigr],

(A.5)

\displaystyle V_{ijz}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2}\,\delta_{ij}\,\bigl(\partial_{z_{i}}-\partial_{z_{j}}\bigr),

(A.6)

\displaystyle V_{izz}(\bm{k}_{2},\bm{k}_{3})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\bm{k}_{2}^{i}-\bm{k}_{3}^{i}\bigr).

(A.7)

z
j
i
\partial_{z_{j}}
\partial_{z_{i}}

Figure 9:
Radial-derivative assignment for the mixed vertex
(
A.6
). When the
i
and
j
legs are external, the
derivatives act directly on the corresponding bulk-to-boundary propagators, giving
(
A.11
). When one or both of these legs is internal, the
derivative is understood to act from the vertex endpoint on the adjacent
transverse bulk-to-bulk propagator.

Four-point vertex.

The quartic Yang–Mills vertex is

V_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\mu\rho}\eta_{\nu\sigma}-\frac{1}{4}\bigl(\eta_{\mu\nu}\eta_{\rho\sigma}+\eta_{\mu\sigma}\eta_{\nu\rho}\bigr).

(A.8)

Here Greek indices run over both spatial and radial directions,

\mu=i,z
, while Latin indices
i,j,k,\ell,m,\ldots
are spatial.
The metric is the Minkowski metric, and we use
\eta_{zz}=1
and

\eta_{ij}=\delta_{ij}
.

Since
\eta_{zi}=0
, the only nonvanishing mixed
z/i
components of

V_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
, apart from the purely spatial
V_{ijkl}
, are

V_{zzij}=V_{zijz}=V_{izzj}=V_{ijzz}=-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{zz}\eta_{ij},\qquad V_{zizj}=V_{izjz}=\frac{1}{2}\eta_{zz}\eta_{ij}.

(A.9)

All remaining mixed components vanish; in particular, any component with an odd number of

z
indices is zero, and
V_{zzzz}=0
.

The propagator factors above should be multiplied by the appropriate external
waves and integrated over the radial coordinates of all vertices.

A.1
Special cases of internal longitudinal propagator

Mixed-vertex radial rule and collapse identity.

We now spell out the elementary identity responsible for the collapse terms
appearing in the main text. In radial space the mixed cubic vertex with one
radial index is represented by the differential operator

V_{ijz}=-\frac{i}{2}\,\delta_{ij}\,\bigl(\partial_{z_{i}}-\partial_{z_{j}}\bigr).

(A.10)

When both legs adjacent to the derivative are external, this operator acts on
the two bulk-to-boundary propagators as

-\frac{i}{2}\bigl(\partial_{z_{i}}-\partial_{z_{j}}\bigr)\Bigl(e^{-k_{i}z}e^{-k_{j}z}\Bigr)=\frac{i}{2}(k_{i}-k_{j})\,e^{-k_{i}z}e^{-k_{j}z},

(A.11)

which reproduces the corresponding momentum-space mixed-vertex factor with

q_{i}=k_{i}
and
q_{j}=k_{j}
.

The same derivative can instead hit an adjacent internal transverse line. For
this purpose it is enough to use the scalar part of the spectral kernel,

G(z_{1},z_{2};k_{s}):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{\sin(p z_1)\sin(p z_2)}{2i\,(k_{s}^{2}+p^{2})}.

(A.12)

Acting with the sum of derivatives at the two endpoints gives

\displaystyle(\partial_{z_{1}}+\partial_{z_{2}})\,G(z_{1},z_{2};k_{s})

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{p\cos(p z_1)\sin(p z_2)+p\sin(p z_1)\cos(p z_2)}{2i\,(k_{s}^{2}+p^{2})}

(A.13)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{p\,\sin\!\bigl(p(z_{1}+z_{2})\bigr)}{2i\,(k_{s}^{2}+p^{2})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{p\,\sin\!\bigl(p(z_{1}+z_{2})\bigr)}{k_{s}^{2}+p^{2}}.

For
L>0
, the standard Fourier integral is

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{\cos(pL)}{k_{s}^{2}+p^{2}}=\frac{e^{-k_{s}L}}{k_{s}},

(A.14)

and differentiating with respect to
L
gives

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{\pi}\,\frac{p\,\sin(pL)}{k_{s}^{2}+p^{2}}=e^{-k_{s}L}.

(A.15)

Setting
L=z_{1}+z_{2}
in (
A.13
), we therefore obtain

(\partial_{z_{1}}+\partial_{z_{2}})\,G(z_{1},z_{2};k_{s})=-\frac{i}{2}\,e^{-k_{s}(z_{1}+z_{2})}.

(A.16)

Equivalently, the same propagator can be written as

G(z_{1},z_{2};k_{s})=-\frac{i}{4k_{s}}\Bigl(e^{-k_{s}|z_{1}-z_{2}|}-e^{-k_{s}(z_{1}+z_{2})}\Bigr),

(A.17)

so
(\partial_{z_{1}}+\partial_{z_{2}})
annihilates the direct piece

e^{-k_{s}|z_{1}-z_{2}|}
and leaves only the image term

e^{-k_{s}(z_{1}+z_{2})}
. This surviving exponential is the
propagator-collapse contribution packaged below into the
operators
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I}
.

A
B
C
D
q

Figure 10:
Schematic rooted-branch configuration for the local operator rule with two mixed vertices sharing a dashed propagator.

After the radial integration, it is useful to package a mixed cubic vertex
(V_{ijz})
with
one radial index as a local operator acting on a rooted scalar skeleton. For
two rooted branches
I,J
meeting at such a vertex, as in
Fig.
10
, define

\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{I,J}:=\frac{1}{4}\Big[(k_{I}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I})-(k_{J}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{J})\Big],

(A.18)

Here
I,J
stand for either the pair
A,B
or the pair
C,D
in
Fig.
10
, and
k_{I},k_{J}
denote the sums
of the energies carried by the outermost external legs of those rooted
branches. The operators

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{J}
are the
propagator-collapse operators associated with the two rooted branches, namely
the extra terms generated when one moves
\partial_{z}
outward so that it acts
on the outermost external leg. Each time the derivative crosses an internal
transverse propagator line, one picks up the corresponding product of
bulk-to-boundary propagators.
Let
\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}
denote the rooted scalar skeleton obtained
after shrinking the dashed line.

For a pair of mixed vertices sharing the same dashed propagator
1/q^{2}
, with
four rooted branches
A,B,C,D
, this becomes

\psi_{(A,B)\,||\,(C,D)}^{\mathrm{sc}}=\frac{1}{4\,q^{2}}\,\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{A,B}\,\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{C,D}\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}.

(A.19)

Expanding the two operators gives

\displaystyle\psi_{(A,B)\,||\,(C,D)}^{\mathrm{sc}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\,q^{2}}\Big[\bigl((k_{A}-k_{B})-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{A}+\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{B}\bigr)\bigl((k_{C}-k_{D})-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{C}+\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D}\bigr)\Big]\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}

(A.20)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\,q^{2}}\Big[(k_{A}-k_{B})(k_{C}-k_{D})\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}-(k_{A}-k_{B})(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D})\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}

\displaystyle-(k_{C}-k_{D})(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{A}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{B})\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}+(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{A}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{B})(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{C}-\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D})\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}\Big].

We have checked this relation against (
A.34
).

Figure 11:
Diagrammatic expansion of the double local-operator rule in
eq. (
A.20
), after shrinking the shared dashed
propagator to a point. A slash on branch
x
denotes the action of the collapse
operator
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{x}
.

A.2
Summary of
n=4,5,6
Feynman diagrams

This subsection records the direct Feynman-rule organization of the

n=4,5,6
gluon wavefunctions reconstructed in
Sections
3.3
–
3.5
.
Wavy internal lines
denote transverse propagation, while dashed internal lines denote the
longitudinal sector. The comparison shows how the polygonal reconstruction
repackages ordinary Feynman diagrams: cut polygons reproduce the transverse and
mixed exchange sectors, whereas the zero-chord terms collect the longitudinal
and contact completions needed for current conservation. We keep the detailed
diagram-by-diagram formulas here so that the main text can focus on the
reconstruction algorithm and the emerging all-
n
pattern.

In this appendix we use
E_{n}:=\sum_{a=1}^{n}k_{a}
for the total energy, and we
reserve rooted symbols such as
k_{\underline{12}}
and
k_{\underline{123}}
for scalar channel
magnitudes only. Whenever a quantity carries a spatial index, such as a
projector or momentum contraction, we write the corresponding vector
explicitly as
\bm{k}_{12}
,
\bm{k}_{123}
, and so on.

Four-point diagrams

At four points, the transverse exchange contribution is obtained by gluing two
triangles. The remaining longitudinal-exchange and contact diagrams combine
into the zero-chord boundary term, collected by the quadrilateral.

k_{\underline{12}}
1
2
3
4

exchange

1
2
3
4

contact

k_{\underline{12}}
1
2
3
4

longitudinal exchange

We define the four-point tubing

{\mathcal{T}^{(4)}(K_{L},K_{R};u):=\frac{1}{(K_{L}+K_{R})(K_{L}+u)(K_{R}+u)}.}

Then

\displaystyle\psi_{4,s}^{\mathrm{YM}}

\displaystyle={V_{12\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu\nu}\,V_{\nu 34}\,\mathcal{T}^{(4)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3}+k_{4};k_{\underline{12}})=\frac{V_{12\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu\nu}\,V_{\nu 34}}{E_{4}\,(k_{\underline{12}}+k_{1}+k_{2})\,(k_{\underline{12}}+k_{3}+k_{4})}},

(A.21)

\displaystyle\psi_{4}^{\mathrm{c}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2E_{4}}\left[(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{3})(\bm{\epsilon}_{2}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{4})-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{4})-\frac{1}{2}(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{4})(\bm{\epsilon}_{2}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{3})\right],

\displaystyle\psi_{4,s}^{(z)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{4})(k_{1}-k_{2})(k_{3}-k_{4})}{4\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}\,E_{4}},

Thus the four-point answer already exhibits the basic split between an
exchange piece carrying the nontrivial channel dependence and a completion
piece proportional to the contact scalar factor
1/E_{4}
.

At five points it is useful to package the two
3\ast 3\ast 3
tubings once and
for all. They are the canonical scalar factors underlying the maximally cut
ray-like sector, and they also reappear in the mixed contributions. For three
rooted blocks with external-energy sums
K_{L},K_{M},K_{R}
, two transverse channels

u,v
, and total energy

E_{5}:=K_{L}+K_{M}+K_{R},

define

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(K_{L},K_{M},K_{R};u,v)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{E_{5}\,(K_{L}+u)\,(K_{M}+K_{R}+u)\,(K_{R}+v)\,(K_{M}+u+v)},

(A.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(K_{L},K_{M},K_{R};u,v)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{E_{5}\,(K_{L}+u)\,(K_{L}+K_{M}+v)\,(K_{R}+v)\,(K_{M}+u+v)}.

{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{\mathrm{scalar}}}:=\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}+k_{5};k_{\underline{12}},k_{\underline{45}})+\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}+k_{5};k_{\underline{12}},k_{\underline{45}}),

(A.23)

{\psi_{5,\,12|345}^{\mathrm{scalar}}}:={\mathcal{T}^{(4)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5};k_{\underline{12}})}.

(A.24)

\displaystyle{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{\mathrm{YM}}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu\nu}\,V_{\nu 3\rho}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{45}}^{\rho\sigma}\,V_{\sigma 45}\,{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

(A.25)

\displaystyle{\psi_{5,\,12|345}^{\mathrm{YM}}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu\nu}\,V_{\nu 345}\,{\psi_{5,\,12|345}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

so the purely transverse sector consists of the
3\ast 3\ast 3
topology and the

3\ast 4
topology. This is the first multiplicity at which two distinct
gluing sectors coexist: a fully triangulated sector built from maximal-cut
data and a one-chord sector whose scalar factor is the four-point tubing

\mathcal{T}^{(4)}
evaluated on the enlarged right block

k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5}
, as in (
A.24
).

\displaystyle{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{(\mu z)}}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{12\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu\nu}\,\bm{\epsilon}_{3\nu}\right)(\bm{\epsilon}_{4}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{5})\left[\frac{(k_{4}-k_{5})(2k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5})}{4\,k_{\underline{45}}^{2}}\right]{\psi_{5,\,12|345}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

(A.26)

\displaystyle{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{(z\mu)}}

\displaystyle=\left.{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{(\mu z)}}\right|_{(1,2)\leftrightarrow(5,4),\,k_{\underline{12}}\leftrightarrow k_{\underline{45}}},

Thus the mixed
3\ast 3\ast 3
graphs do not introduce a new scalar denominator
structure; they reuse the same scalar wavefunction as the
3\ast 4
graph, but
with a different tensor numerator.

{\psi_{5,\,12|3|45}^{(zz)}}=\frac{V_{12z}\,V_{z3z}\,V_{z45}}{k_{\underline{12}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{45}}^{2}\,E_{5}}=-\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!(\bm{k}_{45}-\bm{k}_{12}))\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{4}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{5})\,(k_{1}-k_{2})(k_{4}-k_{5})}{8\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{45}}^{2}\,E_{5}},

(A.27)

which is proportional to the five-point contact scalar factor
1/E_{5}
and
therefore belongs naturally to the completion sector. In this way, the
five-point answer already displays the qualitative pattern that persists at
higher points: the cut-detectable part is generated by a small collection of gluing
topologies, while the completion part is supported on comparatively sparse
longitudinal or contact-like completions.

first longitudinal correction

We first list the all-transverse topologies, then add longitudinal propagators
one by one, and leave the two corrected cases for the end. This makes it
explicit which six-point structures are inherited directly from gluing and
which first appear as local completion terms. Compared with five
points, the novelty is not a different principle but a richer taxonomy of
topologies: ray-like chains, star-like couplings of three lower-point blocks,
and quartic-end or quartic-middle structures that interpolate between them.
This is precisely why six points are useful: they are the first place where
the low-point reconstruction begins to reveal a genuine space of all-
n

building blocks rather than isolated examples. To keep the appendix readable,
we display one representative formula for each topology class; the remaining
cyclic or reflected images are generated by the relabellings already used in
Section
3.5

and summarized in
(
3.36
).
For orientation, the appendix representatives match the main-text sectors as
follows:

We first give the list of scalar functions used to present the six-point
diagrammatic results. Apart from the two maximally cut
\tr\phi^{3}
scalar functions

\psi^{\mathrm{tr}\,\phi^{3}}_{6,13,14,15}
and
\psi^{\mathrm{tr}\,\phi^{3}}_{6,13,35,15}
, the scalar
factors appearing below should be read as lower-point tubing types evaluated
on six-point composite rooted blocks, together with the six-point contact
scalar. The superscripts
(4)
and
(5)
label tubing shapes rather than the number
of elementary external legs; in the six-point applications below, one substitutes

K_{L}+K_{M}+K_{R}=E_{6}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3}+k_{4},k_{5}+k_{6};k_{\underline{12}},k_{\underline{56}})

(A.28)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3}+k_{4},k_{5}+k_{6};k_{\underline{12}},k_{\underline{56}}),

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|36|45}^{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}|_{4\leftrightarrow 6},

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,123|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}^{(4)}(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3},k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6};k_{\underline{123}}),

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|3456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}^{(4)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6};k_{\underline{12}}),

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|3|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6};k_{\underline{12}},k_{\underline{456}})

\displaystyle+\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(k_{1}+k_{2},k_{3},k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6};k_{\underline{12}},k_{\underline{456}}).

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{\mathrm{YM}}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,V_{\nu_{1}3\mu_{2}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{123}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,V_{\nu_{2}4\mu_{3}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{56}}^{\mu_{3}\nu_{3}}\,V_{\nu_{3}56}\,{\psi^{\mathrm{tr}\,\phi^{3}}_{6,13,14,15}},

(A.29a)

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{(\mu\mu\mu)}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,V_{34\mu_{2}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{34}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,V_{56\mu_{3}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{56}}^{\mu_{3}\nu_{3}}\,V_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3}}\,{\psi^{\mathrm{tr}\,\phi^{3}}_{6,13,35,15}},

(A.29b)

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|3|456}^{(\mu\mu)}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,V_{\nu_{1}3\mu_{2}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{123}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,V_{\nu_{2}456}\,{\psi_{6,\,12|3|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

(A.29c)

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{(\mu\mu)}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,V_{\nu_{1}34\mu_{2}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{56}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,V_{\nu_{2}56}\,{\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

(A.29d)

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|36|45}^{(\mu\mu)}}

\displaystyle=V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,V_{\nu_{1}\,3\,\mu_{2}\,6}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{45}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,V_{\nu_{2}\,45}\,{\psi_{6,\,12|36|45}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

(A.29e)

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,123|456}^{(\mu)}}

\displaystyle=V_{123\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{123}}^{\mu\nu}\,V_{\nu 456}\,{\psi_{6,\,123|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}}.

(A.29f)

One longitudinal line

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{(\mu\mu z)}}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,V_{\nu_{1}3\mu_{2}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{123}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,\bm{\epsilon}_{4\nu_{2}}\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{5}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\right)\left[\frac{(k_{5}-k_{6})(2k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6})}{4\,k_{\underline{56}}^{2}}\right]{\psi_{6,\,12|3|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}},

(A.30a)

\displaystyle{\psi_{6,\,12|3|456}^{(z\mu)}}

\displaystyle=\left[-\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})\,(k_{1}-k_{2})\,(k_{1}+k_{2}+2k_{3})}{4\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}}\right]\Bigl(\bm{\epsilon}_{3\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{123}}^{\mu\nu}\,V_{\nu 456}\Bigr){\psi_{6,\,123|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}}}.

(A.30b)

The reflected chain class
(z\mu\mu)
is obtained from
(\mu\mu z)
by

k_{\underline{12}}\leftrightarrow k_{\underline{56}}
and
(1,2)\leftrightarrow(6,5)
.
There are also two additional cases,

{\psi}_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{(\mu z\mu)}
and

{\psi}_{6,\,12|34|56}^{(\mu\mu z)}
, given in the later discussion in
Eqs. (
A.34
) and (
A.37
).

Two longitudinal lines

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{(\mu zz)}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,\bm{\epsilon}_{3\nu_{1}}\right)\left[\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{4}\!\cdot\!(\bm{k}_{56}-\bm{k}_{123}))\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{5}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\,(2k_{3}+k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6})\,(k_{5}-k_{6})}{8\,k_{\underline{123}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{56}}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\psi_{6,\,12|3456}^{\mathrm{scalar}},

(A.31a)

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{(z\mu z)}

\displaystyle=\left[\frac{(k_{1}-k_{2})(k_{1}+k_{2}+2k_{3})}{4\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}}\right]\left((\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})\,\bm{\epsilon}_{3\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{123}}^{\mu\nu}\,\bm{\epsilon}_{4\nu}\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{5}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left[-\frac{(k_{5}-k_{6})(2k_{4}+k_{5}+k_{6})}{4\,k_{\underline{56}}^{2}}\right]\psi_{6,\,123|456}^{\mathrm{scalar}},

(A.31b)

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{(\mu zz)}

\displaystyle=\left(V_{12\mu}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu\nu}\,(\bm{k}_{34}-\bm{k}_{56})_{\nu}\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{4})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{5}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\right)\left[-\frac{(k_{3}-k_{4})(k_{5}-k_{6})}{8\,k_{\underline{34}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{56}}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}},

(A.31c)

Here the reflected class
[zz\mu]
is obtained from
[\mu zz]
in the same way.

\displaystyle{\psi}_{6,\,12|34|56}^{(zz)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{4})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{5}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\,(k_{1}-k_{2})\,(k_{5}-k_{6})}{16\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{56}}^{2}E_{6}}

(A.32a)

\displaystyle{\psi}_{6,\,12|36|45}^{(zz)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{4}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{5})\,(k_{1}-k_{2})\,(k_{4}-k_{5})}{8\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{45}}^{2}E_{6}}

(A.32b)

Three longitudinal lines

\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{(zzz)}=-\frac{(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{3}\!\cdot\!(\bm{k}_{456}-\bm{k}_{12}))\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{4}\!\cdot\!(\bm{k}_{56}-\bm{k}_{123}))\,(\bm{\epsilon}_{5}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{6})\,(k_{1}-k_{2})(k_{5}-k_{6})}{16\,k_{\underline{12}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{123}}^{2}\,k_{\underline{56}}^{2}\,E_{6}},

(A.33)

so the fully longitudinal chain is proportional to the six-point contact scalar
factor
1/E_{6}
.

Propagator-collapse effects and one longitudinal line

The two remaining six-point graphs are the only ones in this subsection
that are not simply a tensor numerator times overall ordinary scalar tubings.

k_{\underline{12}}
k_{\underline{123}}
k_{\underline{56}}
2
1
3
4
5
6

(\mu z\mu)

k_{\underline{56}}
k_{\underline{12}}
k_{\underline{34}}
5
6
1
2
3
4

(\mu\mu z)

We first consider the
(\mu z\mu)
case.

\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{(\mu z\mu)}=\left(V_{12\mu_{1}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{12}}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\,\bm{\epsilon}_{3\nu_{1}}\right)\left(\bm{\epsilon}_{4\mu_{2}}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{56}}^{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\,V_{\nu_{2}56}\right)\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{\mathrm{sc},\,mzm}.

(A.34)

Here the middle longitudinal propagator contributes only the local factor

1/k_{\underline{123}}^{2}
, so the two mixed cubic vertices share the same bulk radial
coordinate. Defining

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=k_{1}+k_{2},\qquad B:=k_{3}+k_{4},\qquad C:=k_{5}+k_{6},

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle:=k_{\underline{12}},\qquad v:=k_{\underline{56}},\qquad E_{t}:=A+B+C=E_{6}.

The scalar factor can be written as

\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{\mathrm{sc},\,mzm}=\frac{1}{4\,k_{\underline{123}}^{2}}\Big[(k_{3}-A)(k_{4}-C)\,\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}+E_{t}\big((k_{4}-C)T_{1}+(k_{3}-A)T_{2}\big)+C_{0}\Big],

(A.35)

where

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(A,B,C;u,v)+\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(A,B,C;u,v),

\displaystyle C_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(A+u)(B+u+v)(C+v)}.

Equivalently, (
A.35
) can be packaged as a local
operator rule acting on the rooted scalar skeleton

\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}:=\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}=\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(A,B,C;u,v)+\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(A,B,C;u,v).

Let
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{L}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R}
denote the left and
right collapse operators associated with the two transverse kernels adjacent to
the shared dashed propagator, defined by

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{L}\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}=E_{t}\,\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(A,B,C;u,v),\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R}\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}=E_{t}\,\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(A,B,C;u,v),\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{L}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R}\,\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}=C_{0}.

Then the scalar factor becomes

\psi_{6,\,12|3|4|56}^{\mathrm{sc},\,mzm}=\frac{1}{4\,k_{\underline{123}}^{2}}\Big[(k_{3}-A)+\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{L}\Big]\Big[(k_{4}-C)+\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{R}\Big]\psi_{0}^{\mathrm{cs}}.

(A.36)

Expanding (
A.36
) reproduces the four expected
pieces: the main term, two single-collapse terms, and the double-collapse
contact-like term.
The contour prescription follows the transverse bulk-to-bulk measure

dp/\pi
, so each spectral integral is implemented by
2i
times the sum of
upper-half-plane residues. In (
A.35
), the first
term comes from moving both radial derivatives onto external exponentials, the
two
E_{t}
terms come from collapsing one transverse kernel, and
C_{0}
is the
double-collapse contact-like remainder.

We now move to the
(\mu\mu z)
case.

{\psi}_{6,\,12|34|56}^{(\mu\mu z)}=-\frac{(k_{1}-k_{2})(\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\!\cdot\!\bm{\epsilon}_{2})}{k_{\underline{12}}^{2}}\left({V_{34i}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{34}}^{ij}\,\Pi_{\bm{k}_{56}\,j}^{\ \ k}\,V_{k56}}\right){\psi}_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{sc},\,mmz}.

(A.37)

The two transverse branches are contracted through the
product of projectors
\Pi_{\bm{k}_{34}}\Pi_{\bm{k}_{56}}
, while the nontrivial scalar
piece is carried by the shared-radial-coordinate derivatives acting on the two
transverse kernels. With

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=k_{1}+k_{2},\qquad B:=k_{3}+k_{4},\qquad C:=k_{5}+k_{6},

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle:=k_{\underline{34}},\qquad v:=k_{\underline{56}},\qquad E_{t}:=A+B+C=E_{6},

the corrected scalar factor is

\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{sc},\,mmz}=\frac{A(B-C+u-v)+2Bu-2Cv}{4\,E_{t}\,(B+u)\,(A+C+u)\,(A+B+v)\,(C+v)\,(A+u+v)}.

(A.38)

Equivalently, using the generic five-point tubings from
(
A.22
) with the identification

(K_{L},K_{M},K_{R};u,v)=(B,A,C;u,v),

one has

\displaystyle\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{sc},\,mmz}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Big[(B-C)\,\psi_{6,\,12|34|56}^{\mathrm{scalar}}+E_{t}\Big(\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(B,A,C;u,v)-\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(B,A,C;u,v)\Big)\Big]

(A.39)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\Big[(u-C)\,\mathcal{T}_{1}^{(5)}(B,A,C;u,v)+(B-v)\,\mathcal{T}_{2}^{(5)}(B,A,C;u,v)\Big].

In (
A.39
), the first term is the expected
effective five-point tubing combination, while the second term is the
propagator-collapse correction generated when the radial derivative is
moved through one of the two transverse kernels.

Taken together, these formulas provide the direct Feynman-rule checks of the
reconstructed answers in Sections
3.3
–
3.5
.
They also make visible, diagram by diagram, the
gluing/completion organization discussed there: transverse and mixed exchange
topologies account for the cut-detectable part, while longitudinal and contact
topologies supply the sparse completion required by current conservation.
